The soundness of this round is established by the next claim.

Claim 8.3
.

Suppose the initial state is doomed, meaning

{\sf agr}(Q,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[(\sqrt{d},\sqrt{d}),\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\;|\;h])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}.

Then for any
g_{1},{\sf Fill}
that the prover sends, we have that

{\sf agr}(F,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[(\sqrt{d},\sqrt{d}),\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}]\leq\varepsilon_{k-1},

with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda}
.

Proof.

Fix any
g_{1},{\sf Fill}
.
By
Lemma

5.8
we have that
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
have correlated agreement at most
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}+\frac{T_{k}+1}{q}
with degrees
(\sqrt{d}-T_{k}-1,\sqrt{d})
and
(\sqrt{d}-T_{k}-1,\sqrt{d})
respectively.
Applying
Lemma

5.9
we get that with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda}
, the function
F={\sf combine}_{(\sqrt{d},\sqrt{d})}(g_{1},g_{2})
has agreement at most
1.04(\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}+\frac{T_{k}+1}{q})\leq\varepsilon_{k-1}
with degree
(\sqrt{d},\sqrt{d})
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
.
∎

The remainder of the round-by-round soundness follows from that of
iRM-IOPP
. Indeed, if (
11
) holds and the state is doomed prior to step
4
, then we are in the doomed initial state of the IOPP
\texttt{iRM-IOPP}(F,(\sqrt{d},\sqrt{d}),q,\lambda,\varepsilon_{k-1})
as described in
Theorem

7.2
.

8.2.2
Concluding the Proof of
Theorem

7.1

With
RS-Poly
and
RSBatch
, we are ready to complete the proof of the inductive step for
Theorem

7.1
, by plugging both IOPs into
Compile
from
Theorem

6.2
. This also concludes the the proof of
Theorem

7.1
. We summarize this process in the following claim.

Claim 8.4
.

Let
d=2^{2^{k}}
. Assuming access to the IOPP

\texttt{iRM-IOPP}(f,(\sqrt{d},\sqrt{d}),q,\lambda,\varepsilon_{k-1})
satisfying
the
k-1
case of
Theorem

7.2
, the IOP
\texttt{Compile}(\texttt{RS-Poly},\texttt{RSBatch}(\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}),\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1})
satisfies the guarantees of the
k
case of
Theorem

7.1
.

Proof.

The parameters are straightforward to verify by applying
Theorem

6.2
with the guarantees for
RS-Poly
and
RSBatch
given by
Lemma

7.3
and
Lemma

8.2
respectively.
∎

8.3
Compiling
iRMPoly
for
Theorem

7.2

We keep
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}
as in
Equation

10
. In particular, note that

\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}\leq\min\left(0.9\varepsilon_{k-1},\varepsilon_{k-1}-\frac{3}{q}\right).

We next design an appropriate batched code membership IOPP appropriate
for compiling
iRMPoly
from
Theorem

7.2
.
In the Poly-IOP
iRMPoly
, the prover sends three polynomials:
\widehat{Q}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 2\sqrt{d}}[x,y]
,
\widehat{F}_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq\sqrt{d}}[x]
, and
\widehat{F}_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq\sqrt{d}}[y]
. When compiling
RSPoly
to an IOP, the prover will instead send the functions
Q,F_{1},F_{2}
which are given over
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2},\mathbb{F}_{q},
and
\mathbb{F}_{q}
respectively. These oracle functions are supposedly the evaluations of
\widehat{Q},\widehat{F}_{1},
and
\widehat{F}_{2}
respectively. While simulating
iRM-Poly
the verifier will query
\widehat{Q}
at
T_{k}+3
and each of
\widehat{F}_{1}
and
\widehat{F}_{2}
at
2
points. Let
A_{Q}\times B_{Q}
, be a subcube containing the side condition points for
\widehat{Q}
. Note that we may have
|A_{Q}|=|B_{Q}|=T_{k}+3
. Let
h_{Q},h_{1},h_{2}
be the side condition functions for
\widehat{Q},\widehat{F}_{1},\widehat{F}_{2}
, i.e. each side condition function maps the verifier’s queries to the prover’s answers for that respective polynomial. Let
\widehat{h}_{Q}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq(T_{k}+2,T_{k}+2)}[x,y],\widehat{h}_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 1}[x],\widehat{h}_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 1}[y]
be the low degree extensions after completing
h_{Q},h_{1},
and
h_{2}
respectively after completing them to
A_{Q}\times B_{Q}
,
A_{1}
, and
A_{2}
in agreement with their respective polynomials.

To complete the proof the inductive step and conclude
Theorem

7.2
, it remains to show how to construct the code membership IOPP with side conditions for the oracle functions above with side conditions. Namely, we must construct IOPPs for the following:

1.

Q
to
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\;|\;h_{Q}]
.

2.

F_{1}
to
\RS_{q^{\prime}}[\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{1}]
.

3.

F_{2}
to
\RS_{q^{\prime}}[\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{2}]
.

The batched IOPP is motivated by the following decompositions from
Lemmas

5.2
and
5.7
, saying
that in the completeness case we have

\displaystyle Q(x,y)

\displaystyle=V_{A_{Q}}(x)\cdot g_{Q,1}(x,y)+V_{B_{Q}}(y)\cdot g_{Q,2}(x,y)+\widehat{h}_{Q}|_{\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}}

\displaystyle F_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=V_{A_{1}}(x)\cdot f_{1}(x)+\widehat{h}_{1}|_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}

\displaystyle F_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=V_{A_{2}}(x)\cdot f_{2}(x)+\widehat{h}_{2}|_{\mathbb{F}_{q}},

(12)

for
g_{Q,1},g_{Q,2}\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d}-T_{k}-3,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}]
and
f_{1},f_{2}\in\RS_{q^{\prime}}[\sqrt{d}-2,\mathbb{F}_{q}]
. The batched IOPP is presented below.

1:

P:
The prover sends the following functions

•

\widehat{h}_{Q}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{(T_{k}+2,T_{k}+2)}[x,y]
which agrees with the side conditions for
Q
. In the honest case, this is the low degree extension of
\widehat{Q}|_{A_{Q}\times B_{Q}}
. The prover sends the polynomial by completing the evaluation over
A_{Q}\times B_{Q}
, allowing the verifier to calculate
\widehat{h}_{Q}
via interpolation.

•

\widehat{h}_{1},\widehat{h}_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 1}[x]
. In the honest case, these are the low degree extensions of the side condition functions on
\widehat{F}_{1}|_{A_{1}},\widehat{F}_{2}|_{A_{2}}
respectively.

•

g_{Q,1}:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
.

•

{\sf Fill}_{Q}:X\times Y\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
.

•

{\sf Fill}_{1}:A_{1}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and
{\sf Fill}_{2}:A_{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}

2:

Let
h_{1},h_{2}
be the functions that are the evaluations of
\widehat{h}_{1},\widehat{h}_{2}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
respectively. Let
h_{Q}
be the function which is the evaluation of
\widehat{h}_{Q}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
. Define the following functions over
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
,
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and
\mathbb{F}_{q}
respectively

\displaystyle g_{Q,2}

\displaystyle={\sf Quo}_{2}(Q-V_{X}\cdot g_{Q,1}-h_{Q},Y,{\sf Fill}_{Q}),

\displaystyle f_{1}

\displaystyle={\sf Quo}(F_{1}-h_{1},A_{1}),

\displaystyle f_{2}

\displaystyle={\sf Quo}(F_{2}-h_{2},A_{2}).

In the honest case, all of the functions appearing are as in (
12
).

3:

V:
The verifier does the following:

•

Choose coefficients according to the proximity generator to obtain

G={\sf combine}_{2\sqrt{d}}(g_{Q,1},g_{Q,2}).

They send the random coefficients in
{\sf combine}
to the prover.

•

Choosing
T_{k}
lines,
L_{1},\ldots,L_{T_{k}}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
and also generates

F={\sf combine}_{2\sqrt{d}}(G|_{L_{1}},\ldots,G|_{L_{T_{k}}},f_{1},f_{2})

according to
Definition

4.6
. They send the lines and the random coefficients used in
{\sf combine}
to the prover.

4:

P + V:
Both Parties run
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(F,2\sqrt{d},\lambda,\varepsilon_{k-1})
.

IOP 4

Batched IOPP for compiling
iRM-Poly
:
iRMBatch

We will now show that
IOP

4
satisfies the requirements outlined in
Theorem

6.2
.

Lemma 8.5
.

The IOPP,
iRMBatch
has the following guarantees.

•

Input:
functions
Q:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
,
F_{1}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
, and
F_{2}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
, as well as their respective side condition functions
h_{Q},h_{1},
and
h_{2}
as described above.

•

Completeness:
if
Q\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\;|\;h_{Q}]
,
F_{1}\in\RS_{q^{\prime}}[\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{1}]
and
F_{2}\in\RS_{q^{\prime}}[\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{2}]
, then the honest prover makes the verifier accept with probability
1
.

•

Round-by-round soundness:

2^{-\lambda}
.

•

Initial state:
the initial state is doomed if and only if one of the following holds

–

{\sf agr}(Q,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[2d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\;|\;h_{Q}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}
,

–

{\sf agr}(F_{1},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[d,\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{1}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}
,

–

{\sf agr}(F_{2},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[d,\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{2}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}.

•

Round complexity:

O(k)
.

•

Input query complexity:

T_{k}\cdot O({\sf inpQ}_{\RS,k-1})
.

•

Proof query complexity:

{\sf pfQ}_{\RS,k-1}+T_{k}\cdot O({\sf inpQ}_{\RS,k-1})+O(T_{k}^{2})
.

•

Length:

O(k\cdot q^{2})
.

Proof.

We discuss all of the guarantees except for round-by-round soundness, which we defer for the next section. The round complexity, input query complexity, and proof query complexity are clear. For the proof complexity, the verifier makes
O(T_{k}^{2})
proof queries to read all of the side condition functions and
{\sf pfQ}_{\RS,k-1}
proof queries when running
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(F,2\sqrt{d},\lambda,\varepsilon_{k-1})
in step 4. Finally, each input query to
F
when running
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(F,2\sqrt{d},\lambda,\varepsilon_{k-1})
is simulated via
O(1)
proof queries. We analyze the completeness below.

In step 1, the honest prover completes the side condition function
\widehat{h}_{Q}
according to
\widehat{Q}
and similarly completes
\widehat{h}_{1},\widehat{h}_{2}
according to
\widehat{F}_{1}
,
\widehat{F}_{2}
. By
Lemmas

5.2
and
5.7
, the honest prover can send
g_{Q,1},{\sf Fill}_{Q},{\sf Fill}_{1}
, and
{\sf Fill}_{2}
which result in
g_{Q,2}\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d}-T_{k}-1),\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}]
and
f_{1},f_{2}\in\RS[\sqrt{d}-2,\mathbb{F}_{q}]
. It follows that
G={\sf combine}_{2\sqrt{d}}(g_{1},g_{2})\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d}]
, and thus for any line
L\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
, we have
G|_{L}\in\RS_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}]
. As a result,
F={\sf combine}_{2\sqrt{d}}(G|_{L_{1}},\ldots,G|_{L_{T_{k}}},f_{1},f_{2})\in\RS_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}]
with probability
1
and the remainder of the completeness follows from that of
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(F,2\sqrt{d},\lambda,\varepsilon_{k-1})
.
∎

8.3.1
Round-by-round Soundness for
Theorem

7.2

There is only one round of interaction, after which we define the state as doomed if and only if

{\sf agr}(F,\RS_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}])\leq\varepsilon_{k-1}.

Now suppose that the state is doomed before this round of interaction. Then, one of the following holds:

\displaystyle{\sf agr}(Q,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[2d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\;|\;h_{Q}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1},

\displaystyle{\sf agr}(F_{1},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[d,\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{1}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1},

\displaystyle{\sf agr}(F_{2},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[d,\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{2}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1},

The soundness of the first round then follows from the next lemma combined with
Lemma

4.8
.

Lemma 8.6
.

If any of the following hold

\displaystyle{\sf agr}(Q,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[2d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\;|\;h_{Q}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1},

\displaystyle{\sf agr}(F_{1},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[d,\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{1}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1},

\displaystyle{\sf agr}(F_{2},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[d,\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{2}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1},

Then, for any
g_{1},{\sf Fill}_{Q},{\sf Fill}_{1},{\sf Fill}_{2}
that the prover sends, we have,

\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{L_{1},\ldots,L_{K}}\left[\Pr_{\xi_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}}[{\sf agr}(F,\RS_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}])>\varepsilon_{k-1}]\right]\leq 2^{-\lambda-10}.

Proof.

First suppose that either the second or third item holds, and by symmetry assume it is the second, namely

{\sf agr}(F_{1},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[d,\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{1}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}
.
Using
Lemma

5.3
, for any
{\sf Fill}_{1}
that the prover sends we have

{\sf agr}(f_{1},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[d,\mathbb{F}_{q}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}+\frac{3}{q},

where
f_{1}
is as defined in
IOP

4
. In this case, the desired result follows from
Lemma

4.8
.

Now suppose that it is the first item that holds, that is
{\sf agr}(Q,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[2d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\;|\;h_{Q}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}
. It follows from
Lemma

5.10
that for any
g_{1},{\sf Fill}_{Q}
that the prover sends we have

{\sf agr}(G,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d}-|Y|,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}])\leq 1.04\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1},

with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda-10}
. Since
(1.04\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1})^{2}\geq 20(\sqrt{d}/q)^{\tau_{{\sf line}}}
,
Theorem

3.10
implies

\Pr_{L\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}}[{\sf agr}(Q|_{L},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[2\sqrt{d},\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}]\geq 1.06\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}]\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}.

Thus, with probability at least
1-\varepsilon_{k-1}^{{}^{\prime}T_{k}}=1-2^{-\lambda-10}
, we have that for at least one line
L_{i}
,

{\sf agr}(Q|_{L_{i}},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[2d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}]\leq 1.06\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}.

In this case,
Lemma

4.8
says that
F
has agreement at most
1.06\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}\leq\varepsilon_{k-1}
with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda-10}
. The result follows from a union bound.
∎

8.3.2
Concluding the Proof of
Theorem

7.2

With
iRM-Poly
and
iRMBatch
, we are ready to complete the proof of the inductive step for
Theorem

7.2
, by plugging both IOPs into
Compile
. This in turn concludes the the proof of
Theorem

7.2
. We summarize this process in the following claim.

Claim 8.7
.

Let
d=2^{2^{k}}
. Assuming access to the IOPP,
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(f,2\sqrt{d},q,\lambda,\varepsilon_{k-1})
which satisfies the
k-1
case of
Theorem

7.2
, the IOPP
\texttt{Compile}(\texttt{iRM-Poly},\texttt{iRMBatch}(\varepsilon^{\prime}_{k-1}))
satisfies the guarantees of the
k
case of
Theorem

7.1
.

Proof.

The parameters are straightforward to verify by applying
Theorem

6.2
with the guarantees for
iRM-Poly
and
iRMBatch
given by
Lemma

7.4
and
Lemma

8.5
respectively.
∎

9
IOPPs for Constant Rate Reed-Muller Codes

In this section we present our constant rate trivariate Reed-Muller IOPP. Specifically, we want to test the proximity of
f:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
to the code
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}]
, where
q=\Theta(d)
. The IOPP construction operates restricting to a smaller dimensional subspace in a similar manner as our low rate IOPPs; however, the constant rate regime presents additional challenges which we briefly discuss here.

The first difficulty that arises is that we cannot rely on
Theorem

3.10
and look at the restriction of
f
to random lines. This is because we are in the constant rate regime and have to reject
f
that may have agreement as high as
\varepsilon=\Omega(1)
with degree
6d
. Using the bound for the line verus point test in
Theorem

3.10
, we would then need to choose
\lambda/\log(1/\varepsilon)=\Omega(\lambda)
random lines to find one on which
f
is far from degree
6d
, and this is too many. To resolve this, we rely instead on the stronger bound for randomly chosen affine planes in
Theorem

3.11
. This theorem allows us to choose only
O(\lambda/\log(q))=O(\lambda/\log(d))
affine planes to find at least one on which
f
is far from degree
6d
.

From here it may seem that we are in the clear and can simply use the proximity generator theorem and test the proximity of some auxiliary bivariate function
g:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q}
to
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}]
, but notice that we are still stuck in the constant rate setting. Indeed, the code
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}]
has constant rate again. To get out of this setting though, we can take advantage of the fact that
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}]
is bivariate instead of trivariate. Specifically, we can now consider the encoding over some larger field
\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}
and instead test proximity to
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}]
. The upshot is we only suffer length
q_{{\sf enc}}^{2}
, so we can afford to make
q_{{\sf enc}}
slightly larger than
q
. Specifically, by choosing
q_{{\sf enc}}
which is polynomially larger than
q
, but still much smaller than
q^{3/2}
, we can simultaneously achieve low rate and sublinear length (sublinear in
q^{3}
that is)! Once we have reduced to testing proximity to a low rate Reed-Muller code, we can (essentially) apply our IOPPs from
Section

7
.

We now formally state our main theorem, which will require stating some of the parameters mentioned above. Fix a security parameter
\lambda
, degree parameter of the form
d=2^{2^{k}}/6
, and finite fields
\mathbb{F}_{q},\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
such that
\mathbb{F}_{q}
is a subfield of
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and

q^{\prime}=2^{\lambda}{\sf poly}(q)\quad,\quad q>6d\quad

Our input will be a function
f:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and we will be testing proximity from
f
to
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}]
.

Towards the construction of this IOPP, we will also require a second, larger field to be used in constructing the batched IOPP during the compiling step. We let this field be
\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}
, and we require it to be a subfield of
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
. We also choose its size to be polynomially larger than
q
but much smaller than
q^{3/2}
. To be concrete, we set
q_{{\sf enc}}=q^{1.2}
, and also define

\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}=23\left(\frac{6d}{q_{{\sf enc}}}\right)^{\tau_{{\sf line}}/2}.

(13)

As the IOPP of this section is intended for use when the rate is constant, one should think of
q=\Theta(d)
.

The reason we must design a different IOPP here compared to
Section

7
is that in the current setting,
\lambda/\log(1/\varepsilon)=\Theta(\lambda)
, so we cannot afford terms such as
\left(\lambda/\log(1/\varepsilon)\right)^{2}
in our query complexity. This was affordable in the low rate setting, as there, the agreement parameter was inverse polynomial in
q
, and thus
\left(\lambda/\log(1/\varepsilon)\right)^{2}=O\left(\lambda/\log(q)\right)^{2}
, which is often small compared to
\lambda
.

During the compilation step, we will use the IOPP
RS-IOPP
from
Theorem

7.1
when the input is over the field
\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}
, and below we recall its parameters adjusted to our setting:

Lemma 9.1
.

Given a security parameter
\lambda
, a degree parameter
6d=2^{2^{k}}
, and field sizes
q_{{\sf enc}}
and
q^{\prime}
as above, there is an IOPP with the following guarantees.

•

Input:
a function
G:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
, parameters
6d,q^{\prime},\lambda
, as above, and an agreement parameter
\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}
as in
Theorem

7.1
.

•

Completeness:
If
G\in\RS_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}]
then the honest prover makes the verifier accept with probability
1
.

•

Round-by-round soundness:

2^{-\lambda}
.

•

Initial state:
the initial state is doomed if and only if
{\sf agr}(G,\RS_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}])\leq\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}
.

•

Round complexity:

O(\log\log d)

•

Input query complexity:

O\left(\frac{\lambda}{\log(q_{{\sf enc}})}\right)
.

•

Proof query complexity:

O\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\log^{2}(q_{{\sf enc}})}\log\log d\right).

•

Length:

O(q_{{\sf enc}}^{2}\cdot\log\log(d))
.

We refer to this IOPP as
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(f,d,q_{{\sf enc}},\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}})

Proof.

We apply
Theorem

7.1
with degree
6d
and parameters
\lambda,q_{{\sf enc}},q^{\prime},\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}
as above.
∎

Our goal in this section is to construct an IOPP as per the following theorem.

Theorem 9.2
.

Fix degree parameter
6d
and security parameter
\lambda
. Let
\mathbb{F}_{q},\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}},\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
be fields such that

\mathbb{F}_{q}
and
\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}
are subfields of
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
with sizes that satisfy:

q>6d\quad,\quad q^{\prime}=2^{\lambda}{\sf poly}(q)\quad\text{and}\quad q_{{\sf enc}}>6d.

Then for any proximity parameter

\varepsilon\geq 23\left(\frac{6d}{q}\right)^{\tau_{{\sf line}}/2},

there is an IOPP for the degree
6d
Reed-Muller code with the following guarantees:

•

Input:
a function
f:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
.

•

Completeness:
if
f\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}]
, then the honest prover makes the verifier accept with probability
1
.

•

Round-by-round soundness:

2^{-\lambda}
.

•

Initial state:
the initial state is doomed if and only if
{\sf agr}(f,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}])\leq\varepsilon
.

•

Round complexity:

O(\log\log d)
.

•

Input query complexity:

O\left(\frac{\lambda}{\log(1/\varepsilon)}\right)
.

•

Proof query complexity:

O\left(\frac{\lambda}{\log(1/\varepsilon)}\right)+O\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\log^{2}(d)}\log\log d\right)
.

•

Length:

O(q^{3})+O(q_{{\sf enc}}^{2})
.

We call this IOP
\texttt{RM-IOPP}(f,6d,q,q_{{\sf enc}},\lambda,\varepsilon)
.

Similar to the proof in the low rate regime, we first construct a Poly-IOP, and then compile it into a legitimate IOP by constructing a suitable batched code membership test according to
Theorem

6.2
.

9.1
A Poly-IOP for
Theorem

9.2

For the remainder of this section, fix
\varepsilon
to be the proximity parameter from
Theorem

9.2
and set

T=5\left(\frac{\lambda}{2\log(\varepsilon)+\log(q)}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad t=\frac{2\log(q)}{\log(1/\varepsilon)}.

Note that the lower bound on
\varepsilon
in
Theorem

9.2
implies
T=\Theta(\lambda/\log(q))
, and it will be helpful to keep this in mind throughout the section. We start with a Poly-IOP for testing proximity to the constant rate Reed-Muller code.

Lemma 9.3
.

Keeping the notation of
Theorem

9.2
, there is a Poly-IOP,
\texttt{RM-Poly}(f,6d,q,\lambda,\varepsilon)
, that has the following guarantee:

•

Input:
the input is an oracle function
f:\mathbb{F}^{3}_{q}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
.

•

Completeness:
if
f\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}^{3}_{q}]
, then the honest prover makes the verifier accept with probability
1
.

•

Round-by-round soundness:

2^{-\lambda}
.

•

Initial state:
the initial state is doomed if and only if
{\sf agr}(f,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}^{3}_{q}])\leq\varepsilon
.

•

Number of polynomials:

T+1
.

•

Input query complexity:

O\left(\frac{\lambda}{\log(1/\varepsilon)}\right)
.

•

Proof query complexity:

T\cdot(t+3)
.

Proof.

We begin by formally presenting the Poly-IOP:

IOP 5

Total Degree Reed-Muller Poly-IOP:
\texttt{RM-Poly}(f,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon)

1:

V:
The verifier chooses
T
planes,
P_{1},\ldots,P_{T}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}
and sends these to the prover.

2:

P:
The prover sends
\widehat{Q}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{Q}_{T}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{6d}[x,y]
. In the honest case
\widehat{Q}_{i}=f|_{P_{i}}
.

3:

V:
The verifier chooses
T
lines
L_{1},\ldots,L_{T}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
and on each line,
L_{i}
, chooses
t=2\log(q)/\log(1/\varepsilon)
points,
z_{i,1},\ldots,z_{i,t}\in L_{i}
. The verifier checks if

\widehat{Q}_{i}(z_{i,j})=f|_{P_{i}}(z_{i,j})\quad\forall i\in[T],j\in[t].

If any check fails, the verifier rejects. Otherwise, the verifier accepts.

The input query complexity, proof query complexity, and round complexity are straightforward to check. For the completeness, each
f|_{P_{i}}
is a degree
6d
oracle over
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
, so the honest prover can respond with
\widehat{Q}_{i}
which is the low degree extension of
f|_{P_{i}}
to
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{2}
for each
i\in[T]
. It is clear that the verifier will then accept with probability
1
in step 3. The rest
of the argument it devoted to the round-by-round soundness analysis.

We define state functions after each round of interaction and analyze the soundness of each round. After the first round of interaction, i.e. after step 1, the state function is as follows.

State Function 5
.

The state is doomed if and only if for at least
\frac{T}{2}
of the planes
P_{i}
we have

{\sf agr}(f|_{P_{i}},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}])\leq 1.2\varepsilon.

The soundness of the first round is established by the following claim.

Claim 9.4
.

If
{\sf agr}(f,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}])\leq\varepsilon
, then
State Function

5
is doomed with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda}

Proof.

By
Theorem

3.11
we have that,

\Pr_{P\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}}[{\sf agr}(f|_{P},{\sf agr}_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}])\geq 1.2\varepsilon]\leq\frac{100}{\varepsilon^{2}q}.

It follows that
State Function

5
is doomed with probability at least,

1-\binom{T}{T/2}\cdot\left(\frac{100}{\varepsilon^{2}q}\right)^{T/2}\geq 1-\left(\frac{600}{\varepsilon^{2}q}\right)^{T/2}\geq 1-2^{-\lambda}.\qed

After the first round of interaction, i.e. after step 3, the state function is as follows.

State Function 6
.

The state is doomed if and only if for at least one
i\in[T],j\in[t]
we have
\widehat{Q}_{i}(z_{i,j})\neq f(P_{i}(z_{i,j}))
.

The soundness of the second round is established by the following claim.

Claim 9.5
.

If
{\sf agr}(f,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}])\leq\varepsilon
and
State Function

5
is doomed, then
State Function

6
is doomed with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda}

Proof.

We have that
State Function

5
is doomed, and assume without loss of generality that it is
P_{1},\ldots,P_{T/2}
which satisfy
{\sf agr}(f|_{P_{i}},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}])\leq 1.2\varepsilon
. Then, for each
i\in[T/2]
, we have

\Pr_{z\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{2}}}[\widehat{Q}_{i}(z)=f(P_{i}(z))]\leq 1.2\varepsilon.

By
Lemma

2.4
applied to the line-point inclusion graph, we have that with probability at least
1-\frac{100}{\varepsilon^{2}q}
over a line
L\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
the following holds:

\Pr_{z\in L}[\widehat{Q}_{i}(z)=f(P_{i}(z))]\leq 1.3\varepsilon.

Thus, for each
i\in[T/2]

\Pr_{L_{i},z_{i,1},\ldots,z_{i,t}}[\widehat{Q}_{i}(z_{i,j})=f(P_{i}(z_{i,j}))\;\forall j\in[t]]\leq\frac{100}{\varepsilon^{2}q}+(1.3\varepsilon)^{t}\leq\frac{101}{\varepsilon^{2}q}.

It follows that
State Function

6
is doomed with probability at least

1-\left(\frac{101}{\varepsilon^{2}q}\right)^{T/2}\geq 1-2^{-\lambda}\qed

Together,
Claim

9.4
and
Claim

9.5
give the round-by-round soundness of
IOP

5
.
∎

9.2
Compiling
IOP

5
and Proof of
Theorem

9.2

In this section we compile the result of
Lemma

9.3
into an IOP as in
Theorem

9.2
. As mentioned, we will test the bivariate polynomials
\widehat{Q}_{i}
over the domain
\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}
so that the code we are testing proximity to,
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}]
, has low rate.

Note that we do not require any subfield relations between the fields
\mathbb{F}_{q}
and
\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}
. We only require that they are both subfields of the much larger
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
. Therefore there is still a degree preserving identification between
f|_{P_{i}}:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
to some
Q_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
. Specifically, starting from a degree
6d
bivariate function over
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
,
f|_{P_{i}}
, we can first take its low degree extension to, say
\widehat{F}_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 6d}[x_{1},x_{2}]
, and then the only
Q_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
which makes sense is
Q_{i}=\widehat{F}_{i}|_{\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}}
.

We now proceed with the description of the batched IOPP. Define the agreement parameter

\varepsilon_{\sf comp}=0.9\varepsilon_{{\sf test}},

where recall
\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}
is as in (
13
). Note that
\varepsilon_{{\sf comp}}=q_{{\sf enc}}^{-\Omega(1)}
. We keep
q,q^{\prime},d,\lambda,\varepsilon
as before. To compile
\texttt{RM-Poly}(f,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon)
, we need to construct a batched code membership IOPP according to
Theorem

6.2
. Specifically, for each
i\in[T]
, we have a side condition function
h_{i}:{\sf Side}_{i}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
for the queries made to
\widehat{Q}_{i}
in
IOP

5
, and we have to test simultaneously the proximity of
T
functions to the codes
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h_{i}]
. This is done in the batched code membership IOPP of
Lemma

9.6
. It is intended for the input
Q_{i}
’s to be obtained from
f|_{P_{i}}
in the Poly-IOP as described above.

We point out that in the initial state of
Lemma

9.6
below, we make the assumption that
T/2
of the input functions are far from the code with side conditions. In contrast, all prior batched IOPPs only assumed that one of the input functions is far in the doomed case. This stronger assumption is necessary for us to achieve the stated input query complexity in
Lemma

9.6
as well as the stated proof query complexity in
Theorem

9.2
. Unfortunately, the doomed initial state here is not black-box compatible with the compilation requirements stated in
Theorem

6.2
. However, unraveling the proof therein, one sees that
Lemma

9.6
is still sufficient for compiling the Poly-IOP from
Lemma

9.3
and described in
IOP

5
. We go over this analysis in more detail in the proof of
Theorem

9.2
.

Lemma 9.6
.

There is an IOPP, which we call
tRMBatch
, that has the following guarantees:

•

Input:
functions
Q_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and side condition functions
h_{i}:{\sf Side}_{i}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
as described, for
i\in[T]
.

•

Completeness:
if
Q_{i}\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h_{i}]
for all
i\in[T]
, then the honest prover makes the verifier accept with probability
1
.

•

Round-by-round soundness:

2^{-\lambda}
.

•

Initial state:
the initial state is doomed if and only if for at least
T/2
of the
i\in[T]
we have

{\sf agr}(Q_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h_{i}])\leq\varepsilon_{\sf comp}.

•

Round complexity:

O(\log\log d)
.

•

Input query complexity:

O\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\log^{2}(q_{{\sf enc}})}\right)
.

•

Proof query complexity:

O\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\log^{2}(q_{{\sf enc}})}\log\log d\right)
.

•

Length:

O(q_{{\sf enc}}^{2}\cdot\log\log d)
.

To construct
tRMBatch
, we follow a similar strategy to the batched IOPPs of
Section

8
. The main difference is that in the current case, the smallest subcube containing
{\sf Side}_{i}
, has size
t^{2}=O\left(\log(q)^{2}\right)
. Thus, the verifier would have to make
O(\log(q)^{2})
queries to read off the side condition function over a subcube, which is unaffordable to us. Instead, we will take advantage of the fact that by design, most of the side condition points are on the same affine line.

For each
i\in[T]
, let
\mathcal{T}_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}
be an invertible affine transformation which sends the
x
-axis to
L_{i}
. That is,
L_{i}\subseteq\{\mathcal{T}_{i}(\alpha,0)\;|\;\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}\}
. Then note that
\mathcal{T}_{i}({\sf Side}_{i})
is contained in a subcube
A_{i}\times B_{i}
with
|A_{i}|=t+1
and
|B_{i}|=2
. This subcube has size
O(t)
rather than
O(t^{2})
. Consider the function
h^{\prime}_{i}:\mathcal{T}_{i}^{-1}({\sf Side}_{i})\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
given by
h^{\prime}_{i}(x)=h_{i}(\mathcal{T}_{i}(x))
. It is not hard to check that for an oracle function
g
we have,

g\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h^{\prime}_{i}]\;\text{if and only if}\;g\circ\mathcal{T}_{i}\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h_{i}],

(14)

and

{\sf agr}(g,\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h^{\prime}_{i}])={\sf agr}(g\circ\mathcal{T}_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h_{i}]).

(15)

Therefore, at the start of the protocol, the verifier will send the prover invertible affine transformations
\mathcal{T}_{i}
as above, and the parties will perform the equivalent task of testing proximity of
Q^{\prime}_{i}=Q\circ\mathcal{T}^{-1}_{i}
to
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h_{i}])
. From here, the batched IOPP can proceed as in
Section

8
.

Proof of
Lemma

9.6
.

We begin with a formal description of the batched IOPP:

IOP 6

Batched IOP for Total Degree Reed-Muller:
tRMBatch

1:

V:
For each
i\in[T]
, the verifier sends an invertible affine transformation
\mathcal{T}_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}
which maps the
x
-axis to the line
L_{i}
from
\texttt{tRMBatch}(f,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon)
.

2:

From now on let
h^{\prime}_{i}
be as described above and let
Q^{\prime}_{i}=Q_{i}\circ\mathcal{T}^{-1}_{i}
.

3:

P:
For each
i\in[T]
, the prover sends
h^{\prime\prime}_{i}:A^{\prime}_{i}\times B^{\prime}_{i}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
as above that agrees with the side conditions, where
A^{\prime}_{i}\times B^{\prime}_{i}
is the smallest subcube containing
\mathcal{T}^{-1}_{i}({\sf Side}_{i})
. The prover also sends a function
g_{i,1}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
for each
i\in[T]
.

4:

For each
i\in[T]
, let
\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
be the degree
(t,1)
function over
\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}
obtained by restricting the degree
(t,1)
extension of
h^{\prime\prime}_{i}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{2}
to the domain
\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}
. Let

g_{i,2}={\sf Quo}(Q^{\prime}_{i}-V_{A^{\prime}_{i}}\cdot g_{i,1}-\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{i},B^{\prime}_{i},{\sf Fill}_{i}).

Recall that in the honest case we have,

Q^{\prime}_{i}=V_{A^{\prime}_{i}}\cdot g_{i,1}+V_{B^{\prime}_{i}}\cdot g_{i,2}+\tilde{h}^{\prime}_{i},

(16)

g_{i,1}\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d-|A^{\prime}_{i}|,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}]
, and
g_{i,2}\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d-|B^{\prime}_{i}|,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}]
.

5:

V:
For each
i\in[T]
. The verifier
generates
F_{i}={\sf combine}_{6d}(g_{i,1},g_{i,2})
according to
Lemma

4.13
. The verifier also chooses
10
lines,
L_{i,1},\ldots,L_{i,10}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}
, and
\xi_{i,j}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
according to the proximity generator for Reed-Solomon codes in
Theorem

4.1
and sends these to the prover. The verifier sets

G=\sum_{i=1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{10}\xi_{i,j}\cdot F_{i}|_{L_{i,j}}.

6:

P + V:
Both parties run
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(G,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}})
from
Theorem

7.1
.

The round complexity, input query complexity, and proof query complexity are clear. We discuss completeness and round-by-round soundness below.

Completeness:

In the completeness case, the prover sends
h^{\prime\prime}_{i}
which agrees with
Q^{\prime}_{i}
on each
A^{\prime}_{i}\times B^{\prime}_{i}
. As we are in the completeness case, each
Q^{\prime}_{i}\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h^{\prime\prime}_{i}]
, so by
Lemma

3.16
the honest prover may send
g_{i,1}\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d-|A^{\prime}_{i}|,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}],g_{i,2}\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d-|B^{\prime}_{i}|,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}]
which satisfy (
16
). In this case,
F
in step
5
has degree at most
6d
, and the verifier accepts in
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(G,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{k-1})
with probability
1
by the completeness of
RS-IOPP
.

Round-by-round soundness:

At the start of the protocol, there is one (trivial) round of interaction where the verifier sends the invertible transformations in step
1
. The state afterwards is as follows.

State Function 7
.

The state is doomed if and only if at least
T/2
of the
i\in[T]
we have

{\sf agr}(Q^{\prime}_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h^{\prime}_{i}])\leq\varepsilon_{\sf comp}.

By (
15
) we have that as long if the initial state is doomed, then
State Function

7
is doomed. This handles the soundness of the first round of interaction.

After the first round of interaction (step 2), the state function as follows.

State Function 8
.

The state is doomed if and only if

{\sf agr}(G,\RS_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}])\leq\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}.

As the previous state is doomed, there is at least
T/2
of the
i\in[T]
satisfy

{\sf agr}(Q^{\prime}_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h^{\prime}_{i}])\leq\varepsilon_{\sf comp}.

Without loss of generality, let these indices be
i=1,\ldots,T/2
.

For each
i\in[T/2]
, it follows by
Lemma

5.10
that for any
i\in[T/2]
,
g_{i,1}
and
{\sf Fill}_{i}
which the prover sends, we will have

{\sf agr}(F_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}])\leq 1.04\varepsilon_{\sf comp},

with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda+10}
. If this is the case, then
Theorem

3.10
implies that

\Pr_{L\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}}[{\sf agr}(F_{i}|_{L},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}])\geq 1.06\varepsilon_{{\sf comp}}]\leq\varepsilon_{\sf comp}.

Thus, over the random lines
L_{i,j}
for
i\in[T/2],j\in[10]
, we get that with probability at least
1-\varepsilon_{{\sf comp}}^{5T}\geq 1-2^{-\lambda+10}
, at least one of the lines
L_{i,j}
satisfies,
{\sf agr}(F_{i}|_{L_{i,j}},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}])\leq 1.06\varepsilon_{{\sf comp}}
. Assuming this is the case, we have

{\sf agr}(G,\RS_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}])\leq 1.06\varepsilon_{{\sf comp}}\leq\varepsilon_{{\sf test}},

with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda-10}
by
Theorem

4.1
. Union bounding over the three mentioned events establishes soundness for this round.

The soundness for the remainder of the IOPP follows from that of
RS-IOPP
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

9.2
.

The desired IOPP is obtained by compiling the Poly-IOP in
IOP

5
using the batched IOPP in
IOP

6
. Specifically, it is
\texttt{Compile}(\texttt{RM-Poly}(f,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon),\texttt{tRMBatch}(\varepsilon_{{\sf comp}}))
from
Theorem

6.2
.

For the completeness, the prover provides evaluations functions
Q_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
, which become the input to the batched IOPP,
IOP

6
. In the honest case, each input
Q_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
in
tRMBatch
is the unique degree
6d
function whose low degree extension to
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{2}
is the same as the low degree extension of
f|_{P_{i}}
to
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{2}
, where
P_{i}
is the plane chosen in the
IOP

5
. The input query complexity, proof query complexity, and round complexity follow directly from
Theorem

6.2
combined with
Lemma

9.3
and
Lemma

9.6
.

For the round-by-round soundness however, we cannot apply
Theorem

6.2
in a black box manner. This is because
Theorem

6.2
would require a batched IOPP where the initial state is doomed when at least one of the input functions
Q_{i}
satisfies
{\sf agr}(Q_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h_{i}])\leq\varepsilon_{\sf comp}
. In contrast, the batched IOPP we construct in
Lemma

9.3
has a stronger requirement for the initial state to be doomed. There, the initial state is only doomed when at least
T/2
of the
T
input functions
Q_{i}
satisfy
{\sf agr}(Q_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h_{i}])\leq\varepsilon_{\sf comp}
. Fortunately, the weaker round-by-round soundness of
IOP

6
shown in
Lemma

9.6
is still sufficient to get
2^{-\lambda}
round-by-round soundness in the compiled IOP,
\texttt{Compile}(\texttt{RM-Poly}(f,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon),\texttt{tRMBatch}(\varepsilon_{{\sf comp}}))
.

To see this, we must open up the proof of round-by-round soundness in
Theorem

6.2
and
Lemma

9.3
. Suppose that we start from the doomed initial state in
\texttt{RM-Poly}(f,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon)
. The analysis is the same up to step
3
of
Theorem

6.2
, where we instead perform a slightly stronger analysis. This stronger analysis shows that if the state is doomed prior to step
3
, then with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda}
, the verifier will not only reject, but will also find at least
T/2
of the indices
i
where at least one of the checks fails. That is, there is at least one
j\in[t]
such that

\widehat{Q}_{i}(z_{i,j})\neq f(P_{i}(z_{i,j})).

(17)

We can then carry this condition over to the round-by-round soundness analysis of the compiled IOP,
\texttt{Compile}(\texttt{RM-Poly}(f,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon),\texttt{tRMBatch}(\varepsilon_{{\sf comp}}))
. Specifically, each such
i
translates to one
Q_{i}
satisfying
{\sf agr}(Q_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h_{i}])\leq\varepsilon_{\sf comp}
in step (c) of the Decision and Test Phase of
\texttt{Compile}(\texttt{RM-Poly}(f,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon),\texttt{tRMBatch}(\varepsilon_{{\sf comp}}))
. As a result, it is sufficient to give a batched IOPP where the initial state is doomed when least
T/2
of the
T
input functions
Q_{i}
satisfy
{\sf agr}(Q_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{2}\;|\;h_{i}])\leq\varepsilon_{\sf comp}
. Such an IOPP is achieved in
Lemma

9.6
, and thus the final IOPP,
\texttt{Compile}(\texttt{RM-Poly}(f,6d,\lambda,\varepsilon),\texttt{tRMBatch}(\varepsilon_{{\sf comp}}))
, has the desired round-by-round soundness.
∎

10
IOP For
{\sf R1CS}

In this section we prove the main theorem which gives an IOP for
{\sf R1CS}
with round-by-round soundness, thereby establishing
Theorem

1.1
,
Theorem

1.2
and
Theorem

1.3
.
Towards this end we first recall the definition of the NP-Complete language
{\sf R1CS}
.

Definition 10.1

(Rank-One Constraint Satisfaction)
.

An instance of
R1CS
consists of three matrices
A,B,C\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{n\times n}
, with membership given as follows

•

Yes case:

\exists v\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{n}
such that
Av\;\odot\;Bv=Cv
and
v_{1}=1
.

•

No case:

\forall v\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{n}
such that
v_{1}=1
, we have
Av\;\odot\;Bv\neq Cv
.

Here
v_{1}
refers to the first coordinate of
v
, and
\odot
refers to the coordinate-wise product operation.

Fix
n
to be the size of the
{\sf R1CS}
instance and let
\lambda
be a security parameter. To construct the IOP, we will assume that we have the following ingredients.

•

The finite field
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
is of characteristic
2
with size

q^{\prime}\geq 2^{\lambda+10}\cdot n^{14/15},

•

A multiplicative subgroup
H
of
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
with size

|H|=n^{1/3}-1.

•

Subfields
\mathbb{F}_{q},\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}
of
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
with sizes satisfying

q,q_{{\sf enc}}\geq 6\cdot(|H|-1),

and

q^{\prime}\geq\frac{2^{\lambda+10}\cdot\max(q_{{\sf enc}},q)^{4}}{n^{2/3}}

The motivation for introducing separate fields
q
and
q_{{\sf enc}}
is towards the linear length application in
Theorem

1.2
. For this setting it is important that
q_{{\sf enc}}
is polynomially larger than
n^{1/3}
, e.g.
q_{{\sf enc}}=\Theta\left(n^{2/5}\right)
, so we encourage the reader to have this setting in mind. That being said,
Theorem

1.1
holds for any
q
and
q_{{\sf enc}}
as above, so for the purpose of generality the remainder of the section proceeds without making this assumption.

10.1
Multivariate Sumcheck

Towards our IOP for
{\sf R1CS}
we need a Poly-IOP for the sumcheck protocol. In this protocol, the verifier is given as input a polynomial
\widehat{f}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 6d}[x,y,z]
, for some degree parameter
6d
, and wishes to check whether
\sum_{b\in H^{3}}\widehat{f}(b)=\alpha
for some target sum
\alpha
and
a multiplicative subgroup
H
.

Lemma 10.2
.

There is a Poly-IOP with the following guarantees:

•

Input:
a polynomial
\widehat{f}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 6d}[x,y,z]
, a multiplicative subgroup
H\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
of size
d+1
, and a target sum
\alpha
.

•

Completeness:
If
\sum_{b\in H^{3}}\widehat{f}(b)=\alpha
then the honest prover makes the verifier accept with probability
1
.

•

Round-by-round soundness:

2^{-\lambda}
.

•

Initial state:
the initial state is doomed if and only if
\sum_{b\in H^{3}}\widehat{f}(b)\neq\alpha
.

•

Number of polynomials:

O(1)
.

•

Round complexity:

O(1)
.

•

Input query complexity:

1

•

Proof query complexity:

O(1)
.

Proof.

We defer the proof to
Appendix

E
.
∎

10.2
Poly-IOP for
{\sf R1CS}
with Round-by-Round Soundness

We now describe our Poly-IOP for
{\sf R1CS}
, which we later compile into a proper IOP. Let us set
d=|H|-1
henceforth. In our description of the IOP, we assume there is an identification between
H^{3}
and
[n]
. We will think of an assignment to the given
{\sf R1CS}
instance,
v\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{n}
, as a map
v:[n]\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
, which by identification can be thought of as a function
v:H^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
. We will repeatedly view vectors of length
n

as functions over
H^{3}
henceforth. In particular, for given a matrix
M\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{n\times n}
, we consider the vector
Mv
and think of it as a function from
H^{3}
to
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
.

Theorem 10.3
.

There is a Poly-IOP for
{\sf R1CS}
, which we call
R1CS-Poly
, that has the following guarantees:

•

Input:
an instance of
{\sf R1CS}
.

•

Completeness:
If the instance is satisfiable, then the honest prover makes the verifier accept with probability
1
.

•

Round-by-round soundness:

2^{-\lambda}
.

•

Initial state:
The initial state is doomed if and only if the instance is not satisfiable.

•

Number of polynomials:

O(1)
.

•

Round complexity:

O(1)
.

•

Proof query complexity:

O(1)
.

Proof.

The poly-IOP is described in
Algorithm

7
.

IOP 7

Poly-IOP for
R1CS
:
R1CS-Poly

1:

P:
For each
M\in\{A,B,C\}
, the prover sends the following

•

\widehat{f}_{M}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 3d}[x,y,z]
. In the honest case the prover has an assignment to the variables, namely
v
, in mind and each
\widehat{f}_{M}
is the low degree extension of
Mv:H^{3}\xrightarrow{}\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
.

•

\widehat{h}_{1},\widehat{h}_{2},\widehat{h}_{3}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 3d-1}[x,y,z]
. Define
\widehat{h}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 3d}[x,y,z]
by

\widehat{h}=1+x\cdot\widehat{h}_{1}+y\cdot\widehat{h}_{2}+z\cdot\widehat{h}_{3}

In the honest case
\widehat{h}
is the low degree extension
v
. Recall that it is assumed that the assignment to the variables,
v
, has
v(0)=1
, so the prover sends
\widehat{h}_{i}
as above as a decomposition of the low degree extension of
v
– which should have constant term
1
.

•

\widehat{g}_{1},\widehat{g}_{2},\widehat{g}_{3}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 6d-|H|}[x,y,z]
. In the honest case,

\widehat{f}_{A}\cdot\widehat{f}_{B}-\widehat{f}_{C}=V_{H}(x)\cdot\widehat{g}_{1}+V_{H}(y)\cdot\widehat{g}_{2}+V_{H}(z)\cdot\widehat{g}_{3}.

(18)

2:

V.
The verifier chooses a random
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and sends this to the prover. The verifier also chooses a random
b\in\mathbb{F}^{3}_{q^{\prime}}
and checks if

\widehat{f}_{A}(b)\cdot\widehat{f}_{B}(b)-\widehat{f}_{C}(b)=\widehat{V}_{H}(b_{1})\cdot\widehat{g}_{1}(b)+\widehat{V}_{H}(b_{2})\cdot\widehat{g}_{2}(b)+\widehat{V}_{H}(b_{3})\cdot\widehat{g}_{3}(b).

(19)

If the check fails then the verifier rejects and the protocol terminates.

3:

Both parties set
\widehat{w}_{\alpha}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq(d,d,d)}[x,y,z]
to be the low degree extension of the function from
H^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
that maps

i\mapsto\alpha^{i-1},\quad\forall i\in[n]\cong H^{3}.

For each
M\in\{A,B,C\}
, let
\widehat{v}_{\alpha,M}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq(d,d,d)}[x,y,z]
be the low degree extension of the function over
H^{3}
given by the following matrix-vector product:

M\times(1,\alpha,\ldots,\alpha^{n-1})^{T}.

In other words,
\widehat{v}_{\alpha,M}
is the low degree extension of the function that maps

i\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^{n}M_{i,j}\cdot\alpha^{j-1},\quad\forall i\in[n]\cong H^{3}.

4:

P+V:
For each
M\in\{A,B,C\}
, the verifier and prover perform the multivariate sumcheck protocol to check that:

\sum_{b\in H^{3}}\widehat{f}_{M}(b)\cdot\widehat{w}_{\alpha}(b)-\widehat{h}(b)\cdot\widehat{v}_{\alpha,M}(b)=0.

The number of polynomials, round complexity, and proof query complexity are clear. We discuss the completeness here and save the round-by-round soundness for the next section.

In the completeness case there exists
v\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{n}
satisfying

Av\odot Bv=Cv

(20)

and
v_{1}=1
. Viewing
v
as a map from
H^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
with
v(0)=1
, let
\widehat{h}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq(d,d,d)}
be its low degree extension. The honest prover then sends
\widehat{f}_{A},\widehat{f}_{B},\widehat{f}_{C}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 3d}[x,y,z]
which are the low degree extensions of
Av,Bv,Cv
and
\widehat{h}_{1},\widehat{h}_{2},\widehat{h}_{3}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 3d-1}[x,y,z]
which satisfy,

\widehat{h}=1+x\cdot\widehat{h}_{1}+y\cdot\widehat{h}_{2}+z\cdot\widehat{h}_{3}.

In this case,
f_{A}\cdot f_{B}-f_{C}
is indeed
0
over
H^{3}
because of (
20
). Therefore, the honest prover can find
\widehat{g}_{1},\widehat{g}_{2},\widehat{g}_{3}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq 6d-|H|}[x,y,z]
satisfying

f_{A}\cdot f_{B}-f_{C}=\widehat{V}_{H}(x)\cdot\widehat{g}_{1}+\widehat{V}_{H}(y)\cdot\widehat{g}_{2}+\widehat{V}_{H}(z)\cdot\widehat{g}_{3}.

(21)

As a result, in step
2
, for every
b\in\mathbb{F}^{3}_{q^{\prime}}
that the verifier may choose, the equation,

\widehat{f}_{A}(b)\cdot\widehat{f}_{B}(b)-\widehat{f}_{C}(b)=\widehat{V}_{H}(b_{1})\cdot\widehat{g}_{1}(b)+\widehat{V}_{H}(b_{2})\cdot\widehat{g}_{2}(b)+\widehat{V}_{H}(b_{3})\cdot\widehat{g}_{3}(b)

will be satisfied. Finally, since
\widehat{f}_{M}
is the honest low degree extension of
Mv
for each
M\in\{A,B,C\}
, for whichever
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
that the verifier chooses in step 2, it will be the case that

\widehat{f}_{M}\cdot w_{\alpha}(b)=\widehat{h}\cdot v_{\alpha,M}(b),

for all
b\in H^{3}
. Thus, from step 4 onward, the verifier will accept the honest prover with probability
1
due to the completeness of the multivariate sumcheck protocol.
∎

10.2.1
Round-by-round Soundness for
Theorem

10.3

In this section we show the round-by-round soundness of
IOP

7
.
There is only one round of interaction before the parties move onto the multivariate sumcheck protocol. After the prover sends the polynomials in step 1, the state is as follows.

State Function 9
.

The state is doomed if and only if at least one of the following holds:

•

The verifier’s check in (
19
) fails. In this case the verifier rejects and the protocol terminates. Every state afterwards is doomed.

•

For some
M\in\{A,B,C\}
, we have
\sum_{b\in H^{3}}\widehat{f}_{M}(b)\cdot\widehat{w}_{\alpha}(b)-\widehat{h}(b)\cdot\widehat{v}_{\alpha,M}(b)\neq 0
.

For any polynomial
\widehat{f}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}[x,y,z]
, we view
\widehat{f}|_{H^{3}}
as a length
n
vector over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
, and for an
M\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{n\times n}
, let
M\cdot\widehat{f}|_{H^{3}}
be the matrix-vector product.

Claim 10.4
.

If the intial state is doomed, meaning
\{A,B,C\}
is not a satisfiable
{\sf R1CS}
instance, then for any prover message in step 1,
State Function

9
will be doomed after step
2
with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda}
.

Proof.

Note that for any prover messages in step
1
, at least one of the following must hold:

•

(\widehat{f}_{A}\cdot\widehat{f}_{B}-\widehat{f}_{C})|_{H^{3}}\neq 0
.

•

For some
M\in\{A,B,C\}
,
\widehat{f}_{M}|_{H^{3}}\neq M\cdot\widehat{h}|_{H^{3}}
.

Indeed, if not, then one can check that
\widehat{h}|_{H^{3}}
gives a satisfying assignment to the
{\sf R1CS}
instance
\{A,B,C\}
.

Let us suppose that it is the first item that holds then. In this case, (
19
) fails with probability at least
1-6d/q^{\prime}
by the Schwartz-Zippel lemma, and we are done.

Now suppose that it is the second item that does not hold, and fix the
M
for which we have inequality in the second item. Let
\widehat{G}_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq|H|^{3}-1}[x]
be the degree
|H|^{3}-1
polynomial whose coefficients are the entries of the vector
\widehat{f}_{M}
, and let
\widehat{G}_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq|H|^{3}-1}[x]
be the degree
|H|^{3}-1
polynomial whose coefficients are the entries of the vector
M\cdot\widehat{h}|_{H^{3}}
. By assumption
\widehat{G}_{1}\neq\widehat{G}_{2}
. However, note that

\sum_{b\in H^{3}}\widehat{f}_{M}(b)\cdot\widehat{w}_{\alpha}(b)=\widehat{G}_{1}(\alpha)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sum_{b\in H^{3}}\widehat{h}(b)\cdot\widehat{v}_{\alpha,M}(b)=\widehat{G}_{2}(\alpha).

By the Schwartz-Zippel lemma, we have that
\widehat{G}_{1}(\alpha)\neq\widehat{G}_{2}(\alpha)
with probability at least

1-(|H|^{3}-1)/q^{\prime}\geq 1-2^{-\lambda},

and in this case
State Function

9
is doomed by the second item.
∎

We quickly wrap up the soundness in step 4. If
State Function

9
is doomed by its first item, then the remainder of the protocol is doomed and we are done. If
State Function

9
is doomed by its second item, then we are in the doomed initial state of the multivariate sumcheck. The soundness of the rounds therein follow from
Lemma

10.2
.

10.3
A Batched Code Membership IOPP

Towards compiling the IOPP from
Theorem

10.3
to a proper IOP we need to design an appropriate batched code membership IOPP, and we do
that in this section. It is straightforward to check that in
IOP

7
(including the execution of the sum-check protocol in step 4), the prover sends:

•

9
multivariate polynomials
\widehat{G}_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq d_{i}}[x,y,z]
, where
d_{i}\leq 6d
.

•

8
univariate polynomials
\widehat{g}_{j}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq e_{j}}[x]
where each
e_{j}\leq 6d
.

The
9
multivariate polynomials from item
1
are those sent by the prover in step
1
of
IOP

7
. The
8
univariate polynomials from the second item are from the sumcheck in step
4

IOP

7
.

We will encode the
\widehat{G}_{i}
’s over
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}
and the
\widehat{g}_{j}
’s over
\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}
. Recall that using these separate subfields is only important towards the linear length setting in
Theorem

1.2
. We fix following agreement parameters:

\varepsilon_{0}=2\cdot\left(\frac{6d}{q}\right)^{1/2}\qquad\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}=2\cdot\left(\frac{6d}{q_{{\sf enc}}}\right)^{1/2},\qquad\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}=23\left(\frac{6d}{q}\right)^{\tau_{{\sf line}}/2},\qquad\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime}=\left(\frac{12d}{q_{{\sf enc}}}\right)^{\tau_{{\sf line}}/2}.

In particular,
\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}
is large enough to be an applicable proximity parameter in
Theorem

9.2
and
\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime}
is large enough to be an applicable proximity parameter in
Theorem

7.1
.

The maximum number of times any polynomial from
IOP

7
is queried is
2
, so each of the polynomials has a constant sized side condition function. Specifically, for each
\widehat{G}_{i}
,
i\in[9]
we have a side condition function
H_{i}:A_{i}\times B_{i}\times C_{i}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
, and for each
\widehat{g}_{j}
,
j\in[8]
we have a side condition function
h_{j}:A^{\prime}_{j}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
. Here,
A_{i}\times B_{i}\times C_{i}
is a
3
by
3
by
3
subcube containing the points queried to
\widehat{G}_{i}
in
IOP

7
, and
A^{\prime}_{j}
is the set of points queried to
\widehat{g}_{j}
in
IOP

7
. In order to compile
IOP

7
, we must construct a batched code membership IOPP for the codes
\RM_{q^{\prime}}[d_{i},\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\;|\;H_{i}]
, and
\RS_{q^{\prime}}[e_{j},\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}\;|\;h_{j}]
.

In the batched code membership IOPP we will use the IOPPs
\texttt{RM-IOPP}(G^{\star},6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}})
from
Theorem

9.2
and
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(g^{\star},6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime})
from
Theorem

7.1
. We will not recall all of the parameters, but for convenience let us restate their initial states, which will be important for our round-by-round soundness analysis.

State Function 10
.

The initial state of
\texttt{RM-IOPP}(G^{\star},6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}})
is doomed if and only if the input function
G^{\star}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
satisfies

{\sf agr}(G^{\star},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}])\leq\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}.

State Function 11
.

The initial state of
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(g^{\star},6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime})
is doomed if and only if the input function
g^{\star}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
satisfies

{\sf agr}(g^{\star},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}])\leq\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime}.

Using these IOPPs, we construct a batched code membership IOPP satisfying the following.

Lemma 10.5
.

There is a batched code-membership IOPP, which we call
R1CSBatch
, that satisfies the following properties:

•

Input: functions
G_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
with side condition functions
H_{i}:A_{i}\times B_{i}\times C_{i}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
for
i\in[9]
, and functions
g_{j}:\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
with side condition functions
h_{j}:A^{\prime}_{j}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
for
j\in[8]
.

•

Completeness: if
G_{i}\in\RM_{q^{\prime}}[d_{i},\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\;|\;H_{i}]
and
g_{i}\in\RS_{q^{\prime}}[e_{j},\mathbb{F}_{q_{{\sf enc}}}\;|\;h_{j}]
for all
i\in[9],j\in[8]
, then the honest prover makes the verifier accept with probability
1
.

•

Round-by-round soundness:
2^{-\lambda}
.

•

Initial state: the initial state is doomed if and only if
{\sf agr}(G_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[d_{i},\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\;|\;H_{i}])\leq\varepsilon_{0}

for some
i\in[9]
, or
{\sf agr}(g_{j},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[e_{j},\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{j}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}
for some
j\in[8]
.

•

Round complexity:
O(\log\log d)
.

•

Input query complexity:
O\left(\frac{\lambda}{\log(1/\varepsilon_{0})}\right)
.

•

Proof query complexity:
O\left(\frac{\lambda}{\log(1/\varepsilon_{0})}\right)+O\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\log^{2}(n)}\cdot\log\log(n)\right)
.

•

Length:
O(q^{3})
.

Proof.

The IOP is described in
IOP

8
:

IOP 8

R1CSBatch

1:

P:
The prover sends the following functions for every
i\in[9],j\in[8]
:

•

G_{i,1},G_{i,2}:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
.

•

{\sf Fill}_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\times\mathbb{F}_{q}\times C_{i}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
.

•

{\sf Fill}^{\prime}_{j}:A^{\prime}_{i}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
.

2:

For each
i\in[9],j\in[8]
, let
\tilde{H}_{i}:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
be the restriction to
\mathbb{F}^{3}_{q}
of the low degree extension of
H_{i}
to
\mathbb{F}^{3}_{q^{\prime}}
and let
\tilde{h}_{j}:\mathbb{F}_{q}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
be the restriction to
\mathbb{F}_{q}
of the low degree extension of
h_{j}
to
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
. Also let

G_{i,3}={\sf Quo}_{3}(G_{i}-V_{A_{i}}G_{i,1}-V_{B_{i}}G_{i,2}-\tilde{H}_{j},C_{i},{\sf Fill}_{i}),

and

g^{\prime}_{j}={\sf Quo}_{1}(g_{j}-\tilde{h}_{j},A^{\prime}_{j}).

3:

V:
For each
i\in[9]
, the verifier generates

G^{\star}_{i}={\sf combine}_{d_{i}}(G_{i,1},G_{i,2},G_{i,3}),

with starting degrees
d_{i}-|A_{i}|,d_{i}-|B_{i}|,d_{i}-|C_{i}|
, according to
Definition

4.12
. The verifier chooses random coefficients from
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{9}\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
according to the proximity generator from
Theorem

4.4
and sets

G^{\star}=\sum_{i=1}^{9}\xi_{i}\cdot G^{\star}_{i}.

The verifier also generates

g^{\star}={\sf combine}_{6d}(g^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,g^{\prime}_{j}),

with starting degrees
e_{1}-|A^{\prime}_{1}|,\ldots,e_{j}-|A^{\prime}_{j}|
according to
Definition

4.6
.

4:

P+V:
Both parties run
\texttt{RM-IOPP}(G^{\star},6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}})
from
Theorem

9.2
and
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(g^{\star},6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime})
from
Theorem

7.1
.

The round complexity, input query complexity, and proof query complexity are straightforward to see. We discuss the completeness below.

In the completeness case, for each
i\in[9]
there exist
\widehat{G}_{i,1},\widehat{G}_{i,2},\widehat{G}_{i,3}
with degrees
d_{i}-|A_{i}|,d_{i}-|B_{i}|,d_{i}-|C_{i}|
respectively, which satisfy

\widehat{G}_{i}=V_{A,1}\cdot\widehat{G}_{i,1}+V_{B,1}\cdot\widehat{G}_{i,2}+V_{C,2}\cdot\widehat{G}_{i,3}+\widehat{H}_{i}.

(22)

Here
\widehat{H}_{i}
is the low degree extension of
H_{i}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{3}
. The honest prover gives
G_{i,1},G_{i,2}
which are the evaluations of
\widehat{G}_{i,1},\widehat{G}_{i,2}
from (
22
), and
{\sf Fill}_{i}
which is the evaluation of
\widehat{G}_{i,3}
from (
22
). For each
j\in[8]
,
g^{\prime}_{j}
defined in step 2 of
IOP

8
is the evaluation of
\widehat{g^{\prime}}_{j}=\frac{\widehat{g}_{j}-\widehat{h}_{j}}{\widehat{V}_{A^{\prime}_{j}}}
where
\widehat{g^{\prime}}_{j}\in\mathbb{F}^{\leq e_{i}-|A^{\prime}_{i}|}_{q^{\prime}}
. With probability
1
,
g^{\star}
is a function over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
with degree at most
6d
and
G^{\star}
is a function over
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}
with degree at most
6d
. The remainder of the completeness follows from the completeness of
RM-IOPP
and
RS-IOPP
.
∎

10.3.1
Round-by-Round Soundness for
Lemma

10.5

There is only one round of interaction, after which the state is as follows.

State Function 12
.

The state is doomed if and only if at least one of the following holds:

•

{\sf agr}(G^{\star},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}])\leq\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}

•

{\sf agr}(g^{\star},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}])\leq\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime}

Soundness of this round is established by the following claim.

Claim 10.6
.

If the initial state is doomed as described in
Lemma

10.5
, then
State Function

12
is doomed with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda}
.

Proof.

As the initial state is doomed, by
Lemma

5.12
, we know that one of the following holds:

•

For at least one
i\in[9]
,

{\sf agr}(G_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[d_{i},\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\;|\;H_{i}])\leq\varepsilon_{0},

•

For at least one
j\in[8]
,

{\sf agr}(g_{i},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[e_{i},\mathbb{F}_{q}\;|\;h_{i}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}.

Suppose it is the first item, and let
i
be the index for which it holds. Then for any
G_{i,1},G_{i,2},{\sf Fill}_{i}
which the prover sends, by
Lemma

4.13
we have

{\sf agr}(G^{\star}_{i},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[d_{i},\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}])\leq 1.04\varepsilon_{0}

with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda-10}
. Assuming that this is the case, by
Lemma

4.13
, we have

{\sf agr}(G^{\star},\RM_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}])\leq 1.1\varepsilon_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}.

with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda-10}
. Union bounding over these two events, we get the desired soundness in the case that the first item holds.

Now suppose that it is the second item which holds and let
j
be the index for which it holds. By
Lemma

5.3
we get

{\sf agr}(g^{\prime}_{j},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[e_{i}-|A^{\prime}_{i}|,\mathbb{F}_{q}])\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}+\frac{|A^{\prime}_{i}|}{q}\leq 1.04\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}.

It follows from
Lemma

4.8
, that with probability at least
1-2^{-\lambda-10}
we have

{\sf agr}(g^{\star},\RS_{q^{\prime}}[6d,\mathbb{F}_{q}])\leq 1.1\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}\leq\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime}.

This establishes the desired soundness in the case that the second item holds.
∎

Finally, to complete the proof of round-by-round soundness, note that if
State Function

12
is doomed, then the initial state of either
\texttt{RM-IOPP}(G^{\star},6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}})
or
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(g^{\star},6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime})
is doomed in step 4. The remainder of the round-by-round soundness follows follows from the round-by-round soundness of
\texttt{RM-IOPP}(G^{\star},6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}})
and
\texttt{RS-IOPP}(g^{\star},6d,\lambda,\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime})
.

10.4
Proof of
Theorem

1.1

The IOP satisfying
Theorem

1.1
is
\texttt{Compile}(\texttt{R1CS-Poly},\texttt{R1CSBatch})
. The agreement parameters used in compiling are
\varepsilon_{0}
for the trivariate polynomials and
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{0}
for the univariate polynomials. The agreement parameters used in
R1CSBatch
are
\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}
and
\varepsilon_{{\sf test}}^{\prime}
as described in
Section

10.3
.

Using
Lemma

10.5
, one can check that
R1CSBatch
satisfies the requirements of
Theorem

6.2
and that the parameters of
\texttt{Compile}(\texttt{R1CS-Poly},\texttt{R1CSBatch})
are indeed those described by
Theorem

1.1
.

10.5
Proof of
Theorem

1.2

We must apply
Theorem

1.1
with the appropriate setting of
q,q_{{\sf enc}},
and
q^{\prime}
. Specifically, we need
q=\Theta(n^{1/3})
and
q_{{\sf enc}}=\Theta(n^{2/5})
. To achieve this setting, take an integer
s
such that
2^{3s}-1
is divisible by
100
, set
q=2^{3s}
, and take
H\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}
to be the multiplicative of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
of size
\frac{q-1}{100}
. Note that
H
is a multiplicative subgroup of
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
as well. Then, set
q_{{\sf enc}}=2^{5\cdot s}
, and
q^{\prime}=2^{\lambda+c\cdot s}
for some appropriate
s
and large enough, but constant,
c
which makes
\lambda+c\cdot s
divisible by
15s
. It is clear that this setup can be conceived with arbitrarily large
s
and thus can be used for arbitrarily large instances of
{\sf R1CS}
.

10.6
Proof of
Theorem

1.3

We again apply
Theorem

1.1
with the appropriate parameters, but we do not have to be as careful here as we are not concerned with linear length. Take
q_{{\sf enc}}=q=2^{2^{s}}
,
q^{\prime}=2^{2^{s+100}}
, and take
H
to be the multiplicative subgroup of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
of size
2^{2^{s-1}}-1\leq\sqrt{q}
. Once again it is clear that this setup can be conceived with arbitrarily large
s
and thus can be used for arbitrarily large instances of
{\sf R1CS}
.

Appendix A
The Line versus Point Test

Here we give our analysis of the line versus point test. In our setting, both the points and the lines table have inputs from
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, while the outputs are in a larger field
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
which contains
\mathbb{F}_{q}
as a subfield. Recall that
[
HKSS24
]
analyze the
q=q^{\prime}
case. Their proof goes through in the more general setting that we require as well, but we go over the details here for the sake of completeness.

Throughout this section, fix two finite fields
\mathbb{F}_{q}
and
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
such that
\mathbb{F}_{q}
is a subfield of
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
, and let
d<q
be a degree parameter. We let
\mathcal{L}
denote the set of affine lines in the ambient space in either
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
. We only discuss the
m=2
and
m=3
cases. Let
\mathcal{L}_{a}
be the set of affine lines going through the point
a
. We will prove the following two theorems.

Theorem A.1
.

Suppose
f:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and degree
d
lines table
T
satisfy

\Pr_{L\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}}[f|_{L}=T[L]]=\varepsilon,

for some
\varepsilon
such that
\varepsilon^{6}\geq\sqrt{d/q}
. Then, there is a degree
d
oracle function
Q:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
such that

\Pr_{a\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}}[Q(a)=f(a)]\geq\varepsilon-1.01\left(\frac{d}{q}\right)^{\tau_{{\sf line}}}.

Proof.

Deferred to
Section

A.4
, after establishing some preliminary results.
∎

Starting with
Theorem

A.1
, we can use standard boosting techniques to obtain a similar result for all dimensions
m>2
. This is done in
[
HKSS24
]
and comes at the price of some loss in the exponent
\tau
in
Theorem

A.7
. For our purposes, we only need the
m=3
case, so we give a self contained boosting argument, which obtains a better exponent
\tau
for the
m=3
case.

Theorem A.2
.

Suppose
f:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and degree
d
lines table
T
satisfy

\Pr_{L\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}}[f|_{L}=T[L]]=\varepsilon,

for some
\varepsilon
such that
\varepsilon^{6}\geq\sqrt{d/q}
. Then, there is a degree
d
function
Q:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
such that

\Pr_{a\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{3}}[Q(a)=f(a)]\geq 0.49\varepsilon-1.01\left(\frac{d}{q}\right)^{\tau_{{\sf line}}}.

Proof.

Deferred to
Section

A.5
.
∎

A.1
Preliminaries for the Line versus Point Test

We will need the following two lemmas. To present them, we must first define a couple of notions, the first of which is weighted degrees.

Definition A.3
.

For a vector
w\in\mathbb{N}^{m}

and a polynomial
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{q}^{m}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
we define
the
w
-weighted degree of

f
as
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{m}w_{i}d_{i}
where
d_{i}
is the individual degree of
x_{i}
in
f
.

Next, we define discriminants of univariate polynomials.

Definition A.4
.

For two polynomials
A(x)=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{a}A_{i}x^{i}
and
B(x)=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{b}B_{i}x^{i}
, the Sylvester matrix
{\sf Sylvester}(A,B)
of
A
and
B
is defined as the
(a+b)
by
(a+b)
matrix whose first
b
rows are the coefficients vector of
A

(A_{0},\ldots,A_{a},0,\ldots,0)
and its shifts
(0,A_{0},\ldots,A_{a},0,\ldots,0)
up to
(0,\ldots,0,A_{0},\ldots,A_{a})
, and whose last
a
rows are the coefficient vector of
B
,
(B_{0},\ldots,B_{b},0,\ldots,0)
and its shifts
(0,B_{0},\ldots,B_{b},0,\ldots,0)
up to
(0,\ldots,0,B_{0},\ldots,B_{b})
. The resultant of
A
and
B
is defined as the determinant of
{\sf Sylvester}(A,B)
. In the special case that
B=\partial_{x}A
, we denote

{\sf Disc}_{x}(A)={\sf Sylvester}(A,B)={\sf Sylvester}(A,\partial_{x}A)
.

We are now ready to state the two results we need from
[
HKSS24
]
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
S\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{m}
be a set of points and let

\mathcal{Q}_{S,x_{1}}=\{Q\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{m}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]\;:\;Q(a)=0,\;\forall a\in S,\;\partial_{x_{1}}(Q)\neq 0\}.

Then for any weight vector
w\in\mathbb{N}^{m}
, the polynomial
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{S,x_{1}}
of lowest
w
-weighted degree satisfies
{\sf Disc}_{x_{1}}(Q)\neq 0
and is therefore square-free.

Proof.

The proof is given in
[
HKSS24
, Lemma 2.9]
.
∎

Lemma A.6
.

Let
A\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{m+1}[\vec{x},z]
be an
m+1
-variate polynomial and let
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
be a zero of multiplicity one. Then, for every
k\geq 1
, there is a unique
m
-variate polynomial
\Phi_{x}(\vec{x})
which satisfies the following:

•

A(\vec{x},\Phi_{k}(\vec{x}))\equiv 0\mod\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\rangle^{k+1}
,

•

\alpha=\Phi_{k}(0)\equiv\Phi_{k}(x)\mod\langle x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\rangle
,

•

\Phi_{k}
has total degree at most
k
.

Proof.

See
[
Bür00
]
.
∎

A.2
The Basic Decoding Result

The first step towards showing
Theorem

A.1
is to show the following, weaker version in
Theorem

A.7
. It is weaker in
the sense that the guaranteed agreement between
f
and
Q

is smaller:

Theorem A.7
.

Suppose
f:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and degree
d
lines table
T
satisfy

\Pr_{L\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}}[f|_{L}=T[L]]=\varepsilon\geq\Omega\left(\frac{d}{q}\right)^{\tau_{{\sf line}}}.

Then, there is a degree
d
function
Q:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
such that

\Pr_{a\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}}[Q(a)=f(a)]\geq\Omega\left(\varepsilon^{6}\right).

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of
Theorem

A.7
.
We will need the following lemma, analogous to
[
HKSS24
, Lemma 3.1]
, which gives a local to global type statement. At a high level, it says that if for many lines in
L\in\mathcal{L}_{a}
the polynomial
A(L(t),z)\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}[t,z]
has a local low degree root,
T[L](t)
, then
A(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},z)
actually has a global low degree root
P(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})
.

Lemma A.8
.

Let
A(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},z)\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},z]
have
(1,\ldots,1,d)
-degree at most
D
. Suppose there is a point
b\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
such that the following holds:

•

A(b,z)\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}[z]
has no repeated roots.

•

There is a set of affine lines through
b
,
\mathcal{S}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{b}
of relative size
\mu(\mathcal{S})>\frac{D}{q}
, such that for every line
L\in\mathcal{S}
, we have,

A\left(L(t),T[L](t)\right)\equiv 0,

and
T[L](0)=\alpha
, for some
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and all
L\in\mathcal{S}
.

Then there exists
P\in\mathbb{F}^{\leq d}_{q^{\prime}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]
such that
A(x,P(x))\equiv 0
. Moreover,
P|_{L}\equiv T[L]\quad\forall L\in\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

We may assume without loss of generality that
b=0
, as otherwise we can translate
A
. This means that
L(0)=0
for every
L\in\mathcal{S}
. By assumption that
A(0,z)
has no repeated roots, so we may apply
Lemma

A.6
and get that for every
k\geq 0
, there is a polynomial
\Phi_{k}(x)\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}]
satisfying

\Phi_{k}(0)=\alpha\quad\text{and}\quad A(\vec{x},\Phi_{k}(\vec{x}))=0\mod\langle\vec{x}\rangle^{k+1}.

As a consequence,

A(L(t),\Phi_{k}\circ L(t))=0\mod t^{k},\quad\forall L\in\mathcal{S}.

Fix some
L\in\mathcal{S}
and consider
A^{\prime}(t,z)=A(L(t),z)\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}[t,z]
. By assumption
A^{\prime}(0,\alpha)=0
and
A^{\prime}(0,z)
is square-free as a polynomial in
z
. Thus, by
Lemma

A.6
, there is a unique polynomial
\Phi_{L}(t)\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}^{\leq d}[t]
such that
\Phi_{L}(0)=\alpha
and

A^{\prime}(t,\Phi_{L}(t))=A(L(t),\Phi_{L}(t))=0\mod t^{d+1}.

However, note that both
T[L]
and
\Phi_{d}\circ L(t)
satisfy the above. By the uniqueness in
Lemma

A.6
this means

T[L](t)=\Phi_{d}\circ L(t)\quad\forall L\in\mathcal{S}.

To conclude, we must show that
B(\vec{x})=A(\vec{x},\Phi_{d}(\vec{x}))=0
. Since
\Phi_{d}(\vec{x})
has degree at most
d
and
A
has
(1,\ldots,1,d)
-degree at most
D
, we have that
B(\vec{x})
has total degree at most
D
. For every
L\in\mathcal{S}
, let
u
be the direction of the line. We have,
B(u)=A(u,\Phi_{d}(u))=0
. Thus,
B
has more than
D\cdot q^{m-1}
zeroes and therefore
B
is the zero polynomial.
∎

The next theorem gives an interpolation result assuming that
f
and
T
pass the line versus point test with probability
\varepsilon
as in
Theorem

A.7
. In particular, we interpolate a polynomial
A\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}[x,y,z]
which has low weighted degree and other helpful agreement properties with
T
and
f
. We will ultimately apply
Lemma

A.8
to this polynomial
A
to conclude
Theorem

A.7
.

Theorem A.9
.

Fix
\varepsilon
such that
\varepsilon\geq\left(c_{1}d/q\right)^{1/c_{2}}
for some constants
c_{1},c_{2}
. Suppose
f:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and lines table
T
pass the line versus point test with probability
\varepsilon
. Then there is a non-zero polynomial
A\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}[x,y,z]
and a set of points
S\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
such that

•

The
(1,1,d)
-degree of
A
is
O(d/\varepsilon^{2})
,

•

\mu(S)\geq\Omega(\varepsilon^{2})
,

•

For every
x\in S
,
\varepsilon_{x}:=\Pr_{L\ni x}[T[L](x)=f(x)]\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
.

•

For every
x\in S
,
A(x,f(x))=0
.

•

\partial_{z}(A)
and
{\sf Disc}_{z}(A)
are nonzero polynomials.

Proof.

We defer the proof to
Section

A.2.1
.
∎

Applying
Theorem

A.9
, we get that there is a set of points
S\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
of measure
\mu(S)\geq\Omega(\varepsilon^{2})
and a nonzero polynomial
A\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}[x,y,z]
with
(1,1,d)
-degree at most
D=O(d/\varepsilon^{2})
such that the polynomials
\partial_{z}(A)
and
{\sf Disc}_{z}(A)
are nonzero, and the following holds for all
a\in S
:

•

A(a,f(a))=0
,

•

\varepsilon_{a}\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}

From this set
S
, we will find a point
a\in S
which satisfies some useful properties.

Claim A.10
.

There exists a point
a\in S
and
\eta\geq\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})
such that the following holds:

•

{\sf Disc}_{z}(A)(a)\neq 0
,

•

For
\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})
-fraction of lines
L\in\mathcal{L}_{a}
, we have
A(L(t),T[L](t))=0
,
T[L](a)=f(a)
, and
T[L](b)=f(b)
for
\eta
-fraction of
b\in L
.

Proof.

Consider the bipartite graph where the left side consists of all points
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
, the right side consists of all affine lines,
\mathcal{L}
, and the edges consist of all pairs
(x,L)
such that
x\in L
,
x\in S
, and
T[L](x)=f(x)
. Note that by
Theorem

A.9
, we have that each
x\in S
has
\varepsilon_{x}\cdot(q+1)
-neighbors.

For each line
L
, let
\sigma_{L}=\Pr_{x\in L}[(x,L)\in E]
. We have

\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{L}[\sigma_{L}]=\Pr_{x,L\ni x}[(x,L)\in E]\geq\Omega(\varepsilon^{3}).

By an averaging argument, for at least
\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})
-fraction of lines
L
, we have
\sigma_{L}\geq\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})
. Let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
denote these lines. Now we wish to find a set of points
S^{\prime}
that each have many neighbors in
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
. Let
E^{\prime}
be the set of pairs
(x,L)
such that
(x,L)\in E
, and
L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
. We have

\Pr_{x,L\ni x}[(x,L)\in E^{\prime}]\geq\Omega(\varepsilon^{3}).

By another averaging argument, we get that for
\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})
-fraction of points
x
, we have
x\in S
and
\Pr_{L\ni x}[(x,L)\in E^{\prime}]\geq\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})
. We let
S^{\prime}
be the set of such
x
, so that
\mu(S^{\prime})\geq\Omega\left(\varepsilon^{3}\right)
.

We now claim that some
a\in S^{\prime}
is the desired point.
Towards the first item, one can check that
{\sf Disc}_{z}(A)
is the determinant of an
O(1/\varepsilon^{2})\times O(1/\varepsilon^{2})
-matrix whose entries are polynomials in
x,y
of degree at most
D
. Moreover, by the last item of
Theorem

A.9
,
{\sf Disc}_{z}(A)
is nonzero. Therefore,
{\sf Disc}_{z}(A)
has degree at most
O(d/\varepsilon^{2})
, and in particular,

\Pr_{a\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}}[{\sf Disc}_{z}(A)(a)=0]\leq\frac{d}{q\cdot\varepsilon^{2}}<\Omega(\varepsilon^{3}),

where the last inequality holds provided that
c_{2}>6
.
It follows that
{\sf Disc}_{z}(A)(a)\neq 0
for at least one
a\in S^{\prime}
, and we fix such an
a
for the rest of the argument.

Towards the second item, fix
L
such that
(a,L)\in E^{\prime}
. Since
a\in S
, we have
T[L](a)=f(a)
. Now let
B(t)
be the univariate polynomial given by
A(L(t),T[L](t))
. As the
(1,1,d)
-degree of
A
is at most
D
, the polynomial
B
has degree at most
D=O(d/\varepsilon^{2})
. On the other hand, note that
B(t)
is zero at every point
x
such that
(x,L)\in E
. Indeed, for such points we have
T[L](x)=f(x)
and
A(x,f(x))=0
. By construction, there are at least
\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})\cdot q
such points. Since

D=O\left(\frac{d}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)<\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})\cdot q,

we get that
B
must be identically
0
. Finally, we have that
T[L](x)=f(x)
for all
x
such that
(x,L)\in E
, which by construction is at least
\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})
-fraction of
L
. It follows that the conditions of item 2 are satisfied for all
L
such that
(a,L)\in E^{\prime}
, and this establishes the claim.
∎

Having established
Claim

A.10
, we are ready to conclude the proof of
Theorem

A.7

Proof of
Theorem

A.7
.

Suppose
A\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}[x,y,z]
,
a\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
, and
S\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
are as given by
Claim

A.10
. Let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{a}
be the set of lines such that for each
L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
, we have,

A(L(t),T[L](t))=0\quad\text{and}\quad T[L](a)=f(a).

Then by
Lemma

A.8
, we have that there is a degree
d
polynomial
Q:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
such that
Q|_{L}=T[L]
for every
L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
. Since
T[L](b)=f(b)
for
\eta
-fraction of the points
b\in L
, we get that
Q(b)=f(b)
for

\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})\cdot\Omega(\varepsilon^{3})=\Omega(\varepsilon^{6}),

fraction of points
b
.
∎

A.2.1
Proof of
Theorem

A.9

Throughout this subsection,
f:\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}\to\mathbb{F}_{q^{\prime}}
and lines table
T
pass the line versus point test with probability
\varepsilon
.
Our argument for
Theorem

A.9
is essentially the same as the argument in
[
HKSS24
, Theorem 4.3]
. We start with the following claim.

Claim A.11
.

There are two different directions
u,v\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
and a set
H\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}^{2}
such that the following hold:

•

\mu(H)\geq\Omega(\varepsilon^{2}).

•

For every
x\in H
, we have
\varepsilon_{x}\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
, and

T[L(x;u)](x)=T[L(x;v)](x)=f(x).

In the equation above,
L(x;u)
is the line going in direction
u
passing through
f
and
L(x;v)
is defined similarly.

Proof.

The proof is the same as in
[
HKSS24
, Claim A.1]
.
∎

Henceforth, we fix
u,v
and
H
as in
Claim

A.11
and suppose without loss of generality that they are the
x
and
y
axes. Henceforth, for
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{q}
we let
L^{(1)}_{\alpha}
be the line in direction
u
(or parallel to the
x
-axis) through
\alpha
, that is, and let
L^{(2)}_{\alpha}
be the line in direction
y
(or parallel to the
y
-axis) through
\alpha
. In the next lemma, set a constant
\gamma=\Omega(1)
so that
2\gamma\cdot\varepsilon^{2}=\mu(H)
.

Lemma A.12
.

For any
r
such that
\frac{2\log q}{\gamma^{2}}\leq r\leq\gamma\cdot q
, there are subsets
S_{1},S_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{q}
such that:

•

|S_{1}|=r
,

•

|S_{2}|\geq\gamma\cdot q
,

•
